   - G -> H -> I

2. **Indirect Paths:**
   - G -> E -> M -> F -> K -> X -> H -> I

   - G -> E -> L -> M -> F -> K -> X -> H -> I
   - G -> B -> C -> O -> P -> I
   - G -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> O -> P -> I

### Cut Set Analysis:
To disconnect G and I, we need to remove nodes that appear in all paths between them. The minimal cut set can be determined by identifying nodes that are common in multiple paths.

- Removing **H** will disrupt the direct path G -> H -> I and several indirect paths via H.
- Removing **P** will disrupt paths involving P.
- Removing **C** will disrupt paths involving C.

However, by removing just **H** and **P**, we can ensure that all paths between G and I are disrupted.

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes G and I is 2. The cut set can be either [H, P] or [P, C].
